---
name: code-review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

On top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below — a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:

- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.
- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic ("possible Feature Envy"), never a hard violation — and, like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.

Each smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:

- **Mysterious Name** — a function, variable, or type whose name doesn't reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design's murky.
- **Duplicated Code** — the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.
- **Feature Envy** — a method that reaches into another object's data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.
- **Data Clumps** — the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.
- **Primitive Obsession** — a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.
- **Repeated Switches** — the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.
- **Shotgun Surgery** — one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.
- **Divergent Change** — one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.
- **Speculative Generality** — abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn't have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.
- **Message Chains** — long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn't depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.
- **Middle Man** — a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.
- **Refused Bequest** — a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full — the sub-agent has no other access to it.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls — documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

An open-ended artificial-life ecology on WebGPU: closed in matter, open in energy, exact integer physics. Individuals, reproduction and heredity are *inferred* from local rules, never declared (no `Organism` class, no `reproduce()`). Research goal and milestones (M3 bootstrap → M4/M5 ensembles): `docs/plan.md`. Rules in prose plus the arithmetic-bounds argument: `docs/rules.md`.

The repo is a pnpm workspace (TypeScript packages + Vite lab), a Deno toolchain for headless GPU runs, and a Phoenix app (`apps/coordinator`). It is jj-colocated with git (`.jj/`).

## Commands

```bash
pnpm install
pnpm dev                 # lab at http://localhost:5173 (/selftest.html, /island.html)
bin/dev                  # lab + coordinator together (PORT, LAB_PORT override 4000/5173)
pnpm typecheck           # tsc over packages/*/src, apps/lab/src, tests (excludes tests/deno)
pnpm test                # vitest: apps/lab/test, packages/*/test, experiments/test, tools/test
pnpm vitest run packages/sim-ref/test/ref.test.ts -t "<name>"   # single test
deno run -A tests/deno/gpu_golden.ts    # GPU vs CPU reference, bit for bit (native WebGPU)
npx playwright test      # browser golden (tests/e2e), Chrome with WebGPU flags; BL_ALL_ENGINES=1 adds firefox/webkit
BL_E2E_PORT=5241 npx playwright test   # a second worktree on its own port (default 5174; strictPort, never reuses a server)
cd apps/coordinator && mix test         # or `mix test test/coordinator/queue_test.exs`
cd apps/coordinator && mix precommit    # warnings-as-errors, format, test
pnpm gen:prereg          # regenerate experiments/preregistration.md generated sections
pnpm gen:cards           # redraw the link-preview cards (site + one per world) with Chrome/WebGPU; then look at them
```

Run `pnpm install` before any `deno run` in a fresh checkout (deno 2.9 otherwise writes `workspaces` into package.json). Tests that start deno pass `--no-lock` (`tools/test/deno-no-lock.test.ts` checks; a launcher or coordinator a test starts that runs deno itself gets the PATH shim in `tests/deno/lib/no_lock_deno.ts`), but a top-level `deno run`, or a tool that starts deno, still writes a stale deno.lock: when package.json deps change, update it deliberately (`deno cache --frozen tools/stitch.ts` fails while it is stale).

`tests/deno/*.ts` are standalone scripts (not `Deno.test`); run each with `deno run -A tests/deno/<name>.ts`. Several need a real GPU; `coordinator_integration.ts` spawns `mix phx.server` against a scratch data dir.

Experiment CLIs (`tools/`, all Deno, native WebGPU): `run.ts` (write run bundles to `runs/<experiment>/<preset>/<condition>/seed-<n>/`), `analyze.ts` (ensemble analysis), `bootstrap.ts` (M3 MAP-Elites search), `retest.ts`, `calibrate.ts`, `island.ts` (headless island), `stitch.ts` (coordinator segments → bundles). Usage is in each file's header comment and in README.md. `runs/` is gitignored and can hold very large outputs.

## Architecture

**Package imports.** `@bl/*` resolve to `packages/*/src/index.ts` via four separate maps that must stay in sync: `tsconfig.json` paths, `vitest.config.ts` aliases, `deno.json` imports, and `apps/lab/vite.config.ts` aliases. There is no build step for packages.

**Two implementations of one rule set.**
- `packages/sim-ref/src/step.ts` is the executable specification (CPU).
- `packages/sim-gpu/src/shaders.ts` (WGSL) must match it bit for bit. `packages/sim-gpu/src/golden.ts` defines golden cases and runs on any WebGPU host (Deno, browser `/selftest.html`, Playwright).
- `packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts` pins state hashes per `RULE_VERSION` (in `packages/schema/src/config.ts`). Any change to dynamics changes the hashes: an intended rule change bumps `RULE_VERSION` and re-pins deliberately. A change that isn't meant to alter dynamics must leave every pin untouched — new optional `WorldConfig` keys stay absent from `defaultConfig()` so existing configs hash identically.
- All arithmetic is integer (u32 matter, fixed-point controllers, counter-based PRNG keyed on seed/step/cell, stochastic rounding with remainders to the source). Conservation residual is exactly zero. Keep new arithmetic inside the bounds listed in `docs/rules.md` (`MATTER_MAX`, `POOL_MAX`, `mulShr` limits); `validateConfig`/`validateState` enforce them.

**Packages.** `schema` (config, presets, layout, genome, worlds, checkpoint encoding, migration/exchange, M3 founder set) → `sim-ref`, `sim-gpu` → `metrics` (census, tracker, collectives, activity, complexity, stats; runs on readbacks) → `runner` (conditions, `runExperiment`, observers, stitching, island client) and `search` (MAP-Elites, gates). `lineage` (schema, sim-ref) is the lineage inspector's genotype core: keys, ancestry, mutation replay, the controller probe, and the mutation-edge store the lab keeps. `runner` code runs unchanged in browsers and Deno.

**Checkpoints and replay.** A checkpoint artifact carries physics state and observer state together, under one digest. Segmented runs must equal a continuous run byte for byte (`tests/deno/segments.ts`, `tests/deno/stitch.ts`); this determinism is what makes distributed verification possible.

**Archipelago.** `apps/coordinator` (Phoenix, JSON API under `/api`, static status page `priv/static/index.html`) hands islands segment tasks. `Coordinator.Queue` does scheduling, `Coordinator.Segment` owns segment state transitions, and `Coordinator.Store` is content-addressed storage for checkpoints (`.blck`) and bundle files. A fraction of segments is re-run by a second island and compared by state hash. Islands are `apps/lab/island.html` (browser) or `tools/island.ts` (Deno), both via `packages/runner/src/island.ts`. `apps/coordinator/AGENTS.md` has Phoenix/Elixir/OTP conventions to follow there; everything between its `usage-rules` markers is generated by `mix usage_rules.sync` (config in `mix.exs`), so rerun that after dependency changes rather than editing the section. `mix usage_rules.search_docs "<term>" -p <pkg>` searches hexdocs for the coordinator's deps.

**Lab.** `apps/lab/src/sim.worker.ts` owns the WebGPU device, frame loop, rendering and OPFS storage. `apps/lab/src/execution.ts` owns census cadence, observer integrity, checkpoint settlement and replay traversal. State-changing messages run through `exclusive()`, which waits for in-flight frame execution; generation identity invalidates stale readbacks.

**Pre-registration.** `experiments/endpoints.ts` is the source of truth for primary endpoints, held-out observables and activity thresholds, and `tools/analyze.ts` executes it. The generated sections of `experiments/preregistration.md` (between `GENERATED` markers) come from `pnpm gen:prereg`, and `experiments/test/prereg-sync.test.ts` fails when they are stale. Edit `endpoints.ts` and regenerate; never hand-edit inside the markers. The doc is frozen (2026-09-27): its SHA-256 is recorded in `experiments/FROZEN`, `pnpm gen:prereg` now refuses to rewrite the generated sections, and every change goes in a dated amendment section.

**Analysis scale.** Run bundles can be hundreds of MB per run (for example `lineages.tsv`). Analysis tools stream per census rather than loading whole tables; keep new analysis code streaming.

## Codex reviews

Reviews by Codex use **Sol 6.1 at High effort**, not Astra (standing instruction from 2026-10-03, replaces the earlier `gpt-6-astra` policy). Run them read-only at phase boundaries and always before committing:

```bash
codex exec -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort=high -s read-only -C <checkout> - < prompt.md > review.txt
```

Use `< /dev/null` when the prompt is an argument (otherwise codex waits on stdin) and `-o <file>` for the final message. Historical "Astra review" comments in the code are provenance for past reviews; leave them. Delegated agents and workspaces follow the same rule.

+export const sha256Text = sha;
 export const bodySha256 = (body: ProposalBody): string => sha(canonical(body));
 export const proposalId = (body: ProposalBody): string => bodySha256(body).slice(0, 16);
 /** The SHA-256 of a run spec's canonical JSON: two rings that propose the same run are one proposal (the note, "Which rings"). */
# I02 plan: a naturalist that predicts interventions (slice S2, decisions D1, D3, D7)

Stage PLAN (revision 2, after the Sol xhigh plan review; see REVIEW.md), 2026-11-01. Branch `claude/i02-prediction-envelope`
from origin/main 8d577be4. Observer and tooling only: no dynamics change, no `RULE_VERSION` bump, no golden pin moved, no
new `defaultConfig()` key, no model call, no paid compute, no GPU, no deploy, no edit inside the frozen preregistration
GENERATED sections.

## Bounded question

Given a fixed evidence envelope for a small known-history world (registered frame samples, census series, config, an
allowed intervention menu), can a proposer emit a structured, validator-clean probability forecast of one fixed paired
intervention's effect that beats the strongest simple baseline on **held-out whole histories**, in multiclass Brier score
and directional accuracy, with stable calibration?

This slice builds the harness and answers only: does the harness separate an evidence-using rule from baselines, and does a
naive metric get fooled where the scoring does not. It runs no model. Any "better than baseline" statement about a model
is out of scope; the model seam is typed and unrun. No advance/stop decision is evaluable here (restated in EVIDENCE.md).

## Scope cut (review finding 13)

One primary endpoint: a **world-level** readout, **one task per case** (see Task). Deferred: region subjects, births,
multi-readout, cross-seed campaigns beyond fixture qualification, richer calibration analysis, lab Field notes wiring,
bell-ring cases from real bundles, GPU forks via `tools/bench.ts`, the numeric-only vs video-only vs combined arm
comparison (frames are in the envelope; only a test proves the field is reachable).

## What exists and is reused (inspected; corrections from review applied)

- `tools/lib/propose.ts` (`Action`, `Direction` (up/down only), `Prediction` (different semantics, kept), `buildForkSpec`,
  `validateProposalSpec` whose rule is that intervention steps are **strictly after** the fork step C), `tools/lib/bench.ts`
  (paired scoring and sign test conventions), `tools/lib/naturalist-envelope.ts` (the bell baseline header: a different
  "envelope", keep names distinct), `apps/lab/src/naturalist-suggest.ts` (untouched).
- `@bl/runner` `ScheduledIntervention` + `validateInterventions` (lesion; feed with negative amount = drain; sun; zero feed
  is rejected; empty array invalid). `RefSim` does **not** implement `InterventionSim`; `applyLesion(ref, ...)` returns
  void; `applyFeed(state, ...)` (in `@bl/schema`) acts on `WorldState`. The runner has a *private* CPU adapter
  (`runner.ts` ~929-941, feed accounting ~1285-1293) that this slice mirrors in a tool-local adapter.
- `@bl/metrics` census returns components/lineages/living-cell counts, not named scalar readouts; births need tracker
  history. Readouts therefore are defined here from state/census (see Readouts), not assumed.
- "Wound" = lesion; drain = feed with negative `amount`; `injuryShell` configs out of scope.

### Integration point with existing tooling (finding 13)

Concrete: (a) small behaviour-preserving exports from `tools/lib/propose.ts` (canonical JSON + SHA-256 helpers, jar-disc
centre rule) with a test that existing proposal hashes are unchanged; (b) `fromRulesProposal(pred)` adapter mapping the v0
rule proposer's `Prediction` (up/down) into the new schema (probability mass on the stated direction, remainder on
`none`, explicit and versioned), so the v0 rule proposer is a fifth scored baseline on the same envelopes;
(c) result files follow the discovery split of canonical scientific result vs execution metadata
(`packages/schema/src/discovery.ts` ~364-400) and its provenance fields; the discovery runner itself is **not** used (it
supports only specific observers/assays). v0 identities and existing outputs are not modified.

## What is built

### 1. Task and evidence envelope
`PredictionEnvelope` v1 (canonical JSON, SHA-256 id): `caseId`, `historyId`, `familyId` (= source-history family; all
windows and seed variants of one history share it), world `config` (+ `ruleVersion`), `census` series with steps `<= C`
only, `frames`: K registered samples at fixed steps `<= C` as small integer arrays (fixed downsample of cell matter/biomass
bytes; copied, never aliasing sim buffers), the `menu` (allowed operations with bounds), and the **task**: exactly one
frozen tuple `{subject: world, intervention, readout, forkStep C, horizon H}` per case. The envelope holds no post-C data
and no outcome; builder asserts `maxStep <= C`.

Intervention timing: applied at step `C + delta`, `delta >= 1` from the menu (matches `propose.ts` strictly-after rule);
the horizon is measured from C and `H >= delta + settle`.

### 2. Prediction schema (version 1, finding 1 and 2)
One prediction per case, exactly: `caseId`, `envelopeHash`, `task` (must restate the envelope task verbatim; the validator
rejects any other intervention/readout/subject/time, so a proposer cannot pick an easier question), `probs`
`{up, down, none}` in integer per-mille, each 0..1000, **summing to exactly 1000**, `abstain` boolean (abstain requires
`probs` absent), free-text `reason` (stored, escaped, never scored). The displayed direction is `argmax` with the fixed
tie order none > down > up (derived, never submitted). No confidence floor.

### 3. Host validator
`validatePrediction(p, envelope)` -> `{ok, errors[]}`. Rejects: unknown keys, `__proto__`/`constructor`/`prototype` keys,
non-object lines, probs not integers / not summing to 1000 / out of range, abstain with probs, missing probs without
abstain, task != envelope task, wrong `caseId` or hash, duplicate or missing caseIds across a file (file-level check:
exactly one line per case; extras and missing are counted). Task operations convert to a `ScheduledIntervention` passing
`validateInterventions`, **except** the sham (below). Invalid proposals are counted and scored as non-answers, never
repaired.

**Sham = no scheduled operation** (finding 3): `{kind:"none"}` is an explicit validator branch that maps to *omitting* the
schedule, as the existing replay control does. A radius-0 lesion is not a sham (it destroys the centre cell) and a zero
feed is invalid; neither appears in the menu. Sham twin replay must equal the control replay hash at every checked step.

### 4. Proposers (`Proposer {id, propose(env): Prediction}`; take only envelopes)
- `fixedRule`: lesion -> down, feed -> up, drain -> down, sham -> none, with a frozen distribution (e.g. 600/200/200 form).
- `numericTrend`: census-only slope extrapolation of the readout mapped to probs by a frozen rule; ignores frames.
- `shuffledEvidence`: `numericTrend` run on a donor envelope's census. Donors are assigned by a **derangement of family
  ids within each split** (never the same family, never across splits), preserving observation-window shape; assignment
  is frozen into protocol before exclusions or scoring (finding 6).
- Constant/prior baselines (finding 8): `priorMajority` (dev-split class frequencies, applied everywhere), `alwaysNone`
  (prob 1000 none, still a valid forecast), `abstainAll` (coverage floor).
- `mechanismRule` (positive control, **dev diagnostic only**): reads the config (nutrient rate, lesion centre occupancy
  from frame mass) and encodes the known mechanism; labelled as a fixture-aware oracle, never a baseline to beat.
- `v0Rules` via `fromRulesProposal`.
- Model seam: `ModelProposerAdapter { id; propose(env, ctx): Promise<unknown> }` + `adaptModelOutput` routing raw output
  through the validator. No implementation, no network import; a test asserts the module imports nothing outside the repo
  and that no adapter is registered by default.

### 5. Readouts (finding 5)
Versioned `readouts.v1` in protocol.json, evaluated directly from `WorldState` at C+H (no tracker, no census history
dependency): primary world-level readout `livingCells` = count of cells with biomass above the census living threshold
(the same constant the census uses, referenced not copied); secondary reported only as diagnostics: `freeNutrient` (total
nutrient in the field). Each has its own unit and `minEffect` (in its own units, set from **development** seeds before
freeze); effects are never pooled across units. Definitions are cross-checked against `census()` outputs in a test.

### 6. Fixtures with outcomes (finding 4, 12)
Small CPU worlds via `RefSim` (32x32, kernelRadius 4, 3 preset-shaped configs). Tool-local `CpuInterventionAdapter`
wraps clones of `WorldState`/`RefSim`: exact order = advance to `C + delta`, apply operation, advance to `C + H`; logs each
application (requested vs delivered matter, refusals). Every arm gets its own deep clone (test: buffer independence).
Invariants checked separately: energy via `ledgerResidual == 0`; matter via delivered-feed and lesion-destroyed
bookkeeping (the existing ledger checks energy only), with fork-time light/heat counters carried not re-baselined.

Per case: treated twin, control twin (same seed, intervention omitted), and for neutrality an **instrumented vs plain**
pair: identical schedules run with envelope/frame/census construction enabled and disabled; full-state hash equality
checked at every sampled step to C+H (observer neutrality, per the source's instrumented/plain requirement). Outcome =
sign of (treated - control) of the readout at C+H against `minEffect`: up / down / none.

Mechanism fixtures (outcome labels are *measured*, not assumed; any label that disagrees with its mechanism expectation
is reported, not silently relabelled): feed (expect up), lesion on occupied region (expect down), drain (expect down),
sham (none by construction), lesion on empty region (expect none; may differ due to fragmentation or edge effects, so the
dev qualification run decides), and a low-dose feed (expect none by threshold). The earlier "saturated feed is none" idea
is **dropped**: feed has no saturation clamp (`feed.ts` ~77-89).

### 7. Splits and the freeze order (finding 7)
Whole source families by `familyId` hashed with a frozen salt into `dev` / `test` (total histories 12, raised to 24 only if
a dev timing measurement says so, decided before freeze). A separate reserved `qualify` subset of **dev** histories is used
for all fixture qualification, `minEffect` choice, seed-flip stability, runtime measurement and proposer rule tuning.
Freeze (protocol.json hashed into the manifest, written and committed before any test-split outcome is generated):
family list and split membership, salt, seed reservations, `minEffect`, readout definitions, proposer rules/probs, donor
assignments, denominators. Test outcomes are generated once by `predict run --stage confirm`, which refuses to run unless
the protocol hash matches the frozen file. No test case is dropped for unstable outcome: unstable test cases stay in the
unfiltered denominator, flagged `measurementLimited` (the prespecified category), and reported both with and without. Any
post-inspection amendment needs fresh confirmation families and a dated amendment. Proposer code only receives envelopes;
only the scorer reads outcomes. `assertNoLeak` checks disjoint family sets, that no test envelope contains dev data, and
that shuffled donors obey the derangement rule.

### 8. Scoring (`scoreRun`)
- **Multiclass Brier** over `{up, down, none}`: `BS = (1/N) * sum_i sum_c (p_ic/1000 - o_ic)^2`, range 0..2. Abstentions
  and invalid/missing predictions are imputed with the frozen **dev-split prior** distribution for the headline number
  (so abstaining cannot beat the prior), with answered-only Brier also reported.
- Directional accuracy on answered cases (argmax vs outcome), balanced accuracy over outcome classes, **accuracy at matched
  coverage** (each comparator restricted to the cases the least-covering one answered, when that is non-empty),
  coverage, abstention rate, invalid and missing counts, mean absolute effect (own units, reported per unit).
- A **calibrated majority baseline is legitimately strong**: the table does not claim Brier punishes it; "strongest simple
  baseline" is whichever of {priorMajority, alwaysNone, fixedRule, numericTrend, v0Rules} scores best on dev.
- Reliability table over the stated probability of the argmax class, 5 bins with per-bin n shown.
- `validNonNullYield` = valid forecasts on cases whose outcome is not `none` / total cases (renamed from "cost per useful
  experiment"; it is a yield, not a cost). `costPerUseful` = total evaluation cost in simulated cell-steps (all arms,
  nulls and failed attempts included) / count of cases whose outcome is non-null; null when the denominator is 0.
- **Cross-seed effect stability** (renamed from "counterfactual agreement"; no "ceiling"): two complete pairs
  `(treated_s, control_s)` and `(treated_s', control_s')` per qualification case; sign agreement reported. It measures
  heterogeneity across seeds of the same family, not a predictor's accuracy limit. Seed variants share a family/split.
- **Uncertainty unit** (finding 11): the family. Per-family aggregates, whole-family bootstrap with a stated resample
  count/seed, paired differences between proposers on the same families. Intervals are computed only when the test split
  has >= 10 families; otherwise only counts and per-family tables are shown ("descriptive"). Bin and class denominators
  stated; missing-class handling stated.

### 9. Outputs (finding 14)
Under `experiments/prediction-envelope/v0/`:
- canonical, byte-reproducible: `envelopes.jsonl`, `protocol.snapshot.json`, `recipes.jsonl` (config, seed, steps, family:
  full replay inputs), `predictions.jsonl`, `outcomes.jsonl` (with envelope hash and protocol hash), `comparison.json`,
  `comparison.md`, `report.html`, `canonical.sha256` listing hashes of exactly these files.
- non-canonical: `meta.json` (git SHA, a source-closure hash over the tool files and packages used, rule/schema/metrics
  versions, config hashes, commands, hardware, timing, no-model/no-GPU statement). Excluded from the reproducibility diff.
Budget < 1.5 MB; if exceeded, commit `canonical.sha256` + recipes only and say which files were regenerated locally.

### 10. Notebook surface
`report.html` (static, uses `tools/lib/report-html`, no scripts, no external URLs, works from `file://`): per case an
observation block (census sparkline, frame thumbnails), a hypothesis block (probs per proposer), a measured-outcome block
(paired result), visibly distinct; failed predictions first; filter for invalid. Reason text escaped. No lab change.

## Files

Add: `tools/lib/prediction/{envelope,validate,proposers,score,splits,fixtures,adapter,readouts,report-html}.ts`;
`tools/predict.ts` (`build`, `validate <file>`, `run --stage dev|confirm`, `report`; Deno, Node-safe lib);
`tools/tsconfig.prediction.json` (typecheck for the new tool code); `experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.json`;
`experiments/prediction-envelope/v0/*`; tests `tools/test/prediction-{validate,proposers,score,splits,fixtures,report,cli}.test.ts`;
`docs/prediction-envelope-2026-11-01/EVIDENCE.md` (run stage).
Change: `tools/lib/propose.ts` (additive exports only, no behaviour change, hash-identity test);
`docs/naturalist-proposer-v0-2026-10-18.md` (one dated linking line); `package.json` script only if needed.
Not touched: `packages/*/src`, `apps/lab/src`, `experiments/endpoints.ts`, `preregistration.md`, `experiments/FROZEN`,
golden hashes, aliases.

## Tests (named cases; expectations derived by hand, not tuned)

validate: accepts menu lesion/feed/drain/sham; rejects probs summing to 999/1001, float, negative, >1000, abstain+probs,
missing probs without abstain, task mismatch (different readout/intervention/time), extra key, wrong caseId/hash,
`__proto__`, non-object line; file-level duplicate/missing/extra caseIds counted; every non-sham accepted op converts to a
`ScheduledIntervention` passing `validateInterventions`; sham maps to no schedule; radius-0 lesion and zero feed are not
accepted menu entries; invalid never repaired.
envelope: no post-fork data (census and frame steps <= C); hash stable under key order, changes with one census value;
frames are copies (mutating sim buffers after build leaves the envelope unchanged).
proposers: each baseline emits validator-clean output on all fixtures; `fixedRule` ignores census; `numericTrend` ignores
frames; `shuffledEvidence` deterministic, donor family != own family, donor same split, **a later window of the same
history offered as donor is rejected**; `alwaysNone` and `priorMajority` valid; `fromRulesProposal` round trip; adapter seam
rejects out-of-menu raw output; nothing registered by default; module imports nothing external.
splits: whole families never straddle dev/test; all windows and seed variants of a family share a split; deterministic under
input permutation; leak check fails on injected shared family and on a bad donor; confirm stage refuses on protocol hash
mismatch.
score: multiclass Brier hand-computed on a 6-case table; uniform = 2/3, perfect = 0, confidently wrong = 2; majority-class
forecast on an imbalanced set scores as computed (not asserted to be bad); abstain/invalid imputed with dev prior and cannot
beat the prior; matched-coverage comparison; always-`none` exposed on a none-heavy table by balanced accuracy; reliability
bins sum to answered; zero-denominator `costPerUseful` is null; bootstrap resamples families and is skipped below 10.
fixtures (CPU): treated/control equal before the intervention; control omits the schedule; sham equals control; buffers
independent across arms; energy residual 0 and matter bookkeeping closes in all arms; instrumented == plain through C+H;
generation deterministic (rerun hashes equal); trend-confound truth table (hand-specified: rising world, sham and
low-dose feed have paired outcome none) vs a naive before/after scorer applied to a naive `alwaysRising` predictor: the
unit test asserts the **hand-derived** table values, and the pair is reported as a dev diagnostic, not a gate; qualification
report lists measured vs expected labels and seed stability; per-test timeouts explicit.
cli: spawned `deno run --no-lock` smoke test of `validate` and a tiny `run` (2 cases).
report: contains every case id, failed first, escapes reason, no external URLs, no script tags.

## Acceptance gates (runnable)

```
export PATH=~/.nvm/versions/node/v24.2.0/bin:$PATH
pnpm install
pnpm typecheck
pnpm exec tsc --noEmit -p tools/tsconfig.prediction.json
deno check tools/predict.ts
pnpm vitest run tools/test/prediction-*.test.ts
pnpm test                                    # golden-hashes unchanged
git diff --stat origin/main -- packages tests/deno apps/lab/src experiments/endpoints.ts experiments/preregistration.md experiments/FROZEN   # empty
deno run -A --no-lock tools/predict.ts run --stage dev --out experiments/prediction-envelope/v0
deno run -A --no-lock tools/predict.ts run --stage confirm --out experiments/prediction-envelope/v0   # only after protocol commit
deno run -A --no-lock tools/predict.ts run --stage confirm --out /tmp/pe2 && (cd experiments/prediction-envelope/v0 && sha256sum -c canonical.sha256) && diff -r --exclude=meta.json experiments/prediction-envelope/v0 /tmp/pe2
deno run -A --no-lock tools/predict.ts validate experiments/prediction-envelope/v0/predictions.jsonl
```
Runtime: fixture step/case/byte caps and per-test timeouts are measured on the `qualify` subset on identified hardware and
frozen in protocol.json; wall time is never part of a reproducibility assertion. Plus a Sol review (xhigh) of the diff
before any commit.

## Non-goals and limits

- No model, video-only or combined proposer runs; typed seam only. Frames used by no baseline except the `mechanismRule`
  dev oracle and a reachability test.
- Fixtures are small CPU worlds with known mechanisms; nothing is claimed about unfamiliar cases or production presets
  (no neutral-bell recalibration claimed). CPU only; no checkpoint/physics change, so segment/stitch tests do not apply.
- 12 (or 24) families is a harness check, not a power claim; below 10 test families only descriptive output.
- Thresholds, salt, probs and donors are frozen in protocol.json before the first confirm run; changes are dated
  amendments with fresh confirmation families.

## Risks

Small-world outcomes may be mostly `none` or seed-unstable; the qualification step on dev decides which fixture *types*
are kept, and that decision is fixed before freeze (never per test case). If `fixedRule`/prior baselines tie everything,
that is reported as the result.

| # | Sev | Finding | Disposition |
|---|-----|---------|-------------|
| 1 | P1 | Brier on stated-direction probability is not a proper 3-class score | Fixed. Schema now carries `probs {up,down,none}` per-mille summing to 1000; multiclass Brier formula defined; direction derived by argmax with fixed tie order; no 0.5 floor; tests for uniform/perfect/confidently-wrong. |
| 2 | P1 | Proposer can pick an easier question / always-sham wins | Fixed. One frozen task tuple per case in the envelope; validator rejects any other intervention/readout/subject/time; exactly one prediction per case (file-level duplicate/missing check); abstain/invalid imputed with dev prior for headline Brier; accuracy at matched coverage. Experiment selection deferred. |
| 3 | P1 | Sham mapping contradicts runner (r=0 lesion hits centre; zero feed invalid; none not a ScheduledIntervention) | Fixed. Sham = no scheduled operation, explicit validator branch; radius-0/zero-feed excluded from menu; sham replay must equal control hash. |
| 4 | P1 | CPU intervention path/accounting unspecified | Fixed. Tool-local `CpuInterventionAdapter` mirroring the runner's private one; exact order, logs, deep clones per arm; matter and energy invariants checked separately; intervention at `C+delta`, delta>=1 per `propose.ts` strictly-after rule; fork-time counters carried, not re-baselined. |
| 5 | P1 | Readouts/mechanisms undefined; census lacks the fields; saturated-feed has no clamp | Fixed. Single world-level primary readout `livingCells` plus diagnostic `freeNutrient`, defined from state in `readouts.v1`, cross-checked against `census()`; `minEffect` per unit from dev seeds; births/regions deferred; saturated-feed fixture dropped; fixture labels are measured, mismatches reported. |
| 6 | P1 | Shuffled evidence can donate the same history's future | Fixed. Donors assigned by derangement of family ids within each split, frozen before exclusions/scoring; test that a later window of the same history is rejected. |
| 7 | P1 | Dropping cases by seed-flip uses outcomes; freeze order weak | Fixed. Qualification, `minEffect`, tuning and timing on reserved dev `qualify` subset only; everything frozen and hashed before test outcomes are generated; `--stage confirm` refuses on hash mismatch; no test case dropped, unstable ones kept as `measurementLimited` in the unfiltered denominator; amendments need fresh families. |
| 8 | P2 | Negative fixture/baseline assertions unprincipled; no positive control; majority baseline not necessarily bad | Fixed. Thresholds (>=0.8/<=0.34) removed; hand-derived confound truth table and a distinct naive `alwaysRising` predictor; added `priorMajority`, `alwaysNone`, and a dev-only `mechanismRule` positive control; stated that a calibrated majority is legitimately strong; diagnostics, not gates. |
| 9 | P2 | "Cost per useful experiment" is a yield | Fixed. Renamed `validNonNullYield`; `costPerUseful` = total cell-steps (nulls and failures included) / non-null cases; null on zero denominator. |
| 10 | P2 | Seed agreement mislabelled counterfactual agreement/ceiling | Fixed. Renamed cross-seed effect stability; two complete treated/control pairs; "ceiling" dropped; seed variants share family and split. |
| 11 | P2 | Intervals lack independent unit | Fixed. Family is the unit; whole-family bootstrap with stated count/seed; paired differences; intervals only if >=10 test families, else descriptive; bin/class denominators stated. |
| 12 | P2 | Twin equality does not prove observer neutrality | Fixed. Instrumented vs plain runs with independent clones, full-state hash equality through C+H; envelope construction mutation/alias tests. |
| 13 | P2 | Claims extension but builds a separate workflow; discovery format omitted | Fixed. Named integration points: additive `propose.ts` exports (hash-identity test), `fromRulesProposal` making the v0 rule proposer a scored baseline, discovery canonical-vs-metadata result convention; discovery runner explicitly not used and why. Scope cut to one endpoint. |
| 14 | P2 | Byte-reproducibility gate contradicts artifacts; inputs not captured | Fixed. Canonical set (envelopes, protocol snapshot, recipes, predictions, outcomes, comparison, report, `canonical.sha256`) separated from `meta.json`; diff excludes meta only; source-closure hash; size-overflow retention rule. |

     1	// The fixed evidence envelope, version 1 (PLAN.md, sections 1-2). One envelope per case: the world's config, a census series and K
     2	// registered frame samples, all at steps <= the fork step C, the menu of allowed intervention operations, and exactly one frozen
     3	// task. It holds no post-C data and no outcome. Identity is the SHA-256 of its canonical JSON (without the `envelopeHash` field).
     4	import { cellCount, worldW, CH, type WorldConfig, type WorldState } from "@bl/schema";
     5	import { canonical, sha256Text } from "../propose.ts";
     6	import { PRIMARY_READOUT, type ReadoutId } from "./readouts.ts";

     8	export const ENVELOPE_VERSION = 1;
     9	export const PREDICTION_VERSION = 1;
    10	export const CLASSES = ["up", "down", "none"] as const;
    11	export type Direction3 = (typeof CLASSES)[number];
    12	export type OpKind = "lesion" | "feed" | "drain" | "none";

    14	/** One operation. A drain's `amount` is positive (nutrient taken per cell); the scheduled feed carries it negated. A sham has no other key. */
    15	export interface TaskOp {
    16	  kind: OpKind;
    17	  x?: number;
    18	  y?: number;

    22	export interface Task {

    24	  op: TaskOp;
    25	  /** The operation lands at step forkStep + delta (delta >= 1: strictly after the fork, as tools/lib/propose.ts requires). */
    26	  delta: number;
    27	  readout: ReadoutId;
    28	  forkStep: number;
    29	  /** The readout is read at forkStep + horizon. */
    30	  horizon: number;
    31	}
    32	export interface MenuEntry {

    34	  /** Radius bounds (inclusive); radius 0 is not offered (a radius-0 lesion still destroys its centre cell, so it is no sham). */
    35	  rMin?: number;

    37	  amountMin?: number;
    38	  amountMax?: number;

    51	/** A registered frame sample: the world's B+P mass summed over `side` x `side` blocks, row-major (a copy, never an alias of a sim buffer). */

    66	  frames: Frame[];

    72	export const FRAME_SIDE = 8;

    74	export function standardMenu(cfg: WorldConfig, deltaMax: number, amountMax: number, rMax: number): Menu {
    75	  void cfg;
    76	  return {
    77	    ops: [
    78	      { kind: "lesion", rMin: 1, rMax },
    79	      { kind: "feed", rMin: 1, rMax, amountMin: 1, amountMax },
    80	      { kind: "drain", rMin: 1, rMax, amountMin: 1, amountMax },
    81	      { kind: "none" },
    82	    ],
    83	    deltaMin: 1,
    84	    deltaMax,
    85	  };
    86	}

    88	export function censusPoint(s: WorldState, living: number, free: number, mass: number): CensusPoint {
    89	  return { step: s.step, livingCells: living, freeNutrient: free, mass };
    90	}
    91	
    92	/** A frame sample copied out of a state: B+P mass per block. */
    93	export function frameOf(s: WorldState, side = FRAME_SIDE): Frame {
    94	  const n = cellCount(s.cfg);
    95	  const W = worldW(s.cfg);
    96	  const H = n / W;
    97	  const bw = Math.floor(W / side), bh = Math.floor(H / side);
    98	  const mass = new Array<number>(side * side).fill(0);
    99	  for (let y = 0; y < bh * side; y++) {
   100	    for (let x = 0; x < bw * side; x++) {
   101	      const i = y * W + x;
   102	      mass[Math.floor(y / bh) * side + Math.floor(x / bw)] += s.cells[CH.B * n + i] + s.cells[CH.P * n + i];
   103	    }
   104	  }
   105	  return { step: s.step, side, mass };
   106	}

   108	export function envelopeBody(e: PredictionEnvelope): Omit<PredictionEnvelope, "envelopeHash"> {
   109	  const { envelopeHash: _h, ...rest } = e;
   110	  void _h;
   111	  return rest;
   112	}
   113	export const hashEnvelopeBody = (b: Omit<PredictionEnvelope, "envelopeHash">): string => sha256Text(canonical(b));
   114	
   115	export function sealEnvelope(b: Omit<PredictionEnvelope, "envelopeHash">): PredictionEnvelope {
   116	  assertNoPostFork(b);
   117	  return { ...b, envelopeHash: hashEnvelopeBody(b) };
   118	}
   119	
   120	/** Throws unless every census point and frame is at a step <= the fork step. */
   121	export function assertNoPostFork(b: Omit<PredictionEnvelope, "envelopeHash">): void {
   122	  const C = b.task.forkStep;
   123	  const max = Math.max(-1, ...b.census.map((c) => c.step), ...b.frames.map((f) => f.step));
   124	  if (max > C) throw new Error(`envelope ${b.caseId} holds data at step ${max}, after its fork step ${C}`);
   125	}
   126	
   127	export function envelopeIntact(e: PredictionEnvelope): boolean {
   128	  return e.envelopeHash === hashEnvelopeBody(envelopeBody(e));
   129	}

   131	export const taskKey = (t: Task): string => canonical(t);

     1	// The host validator (PLAN.md, section 3). A prediction is accepted only if it is exactly the schema, restates the envelope's one
     2	// frozen task verbatim, and its task converts to operations the existing runner validator accepts. Invalid proposals are counted
     3	// and scored as non-answers; nothing is ever repaired. The sham ({kind:"none"}) is an explicit branch that maps to NO scheduled
     4	// operation (the existing replay control omits the schedule), never to a radius-0 lesion or a zero feed.
     5	import { cellCount, worldW } from "@bl/schema";
     6	import { interventionsError, validateInterventions, type ScheduledIntervention } from "@bl/runner";
     7	import { canonical } from "../propose.ts";
     8	import { CLASSES, ENVELOPE_VERSION, PREDICTION_VERSION, envelopeIntact, type Direction3, type PredictionEnvelope, type Task } from "./envelope.ts";
     9	
    10	export interface Probs {
    11	  up: number;
    12	  down: number;
    13	  none: number;
    14	}
    15	export interface Prediction {
    16	  version: number;
    17	  caseId: string;
    18	  envelopeHash: string;
    19	  task: Task;
    20	  probs?: Probs;
    21	  abstain: boolean;
    22	  reason?: string;
    23	}
    24	export interface Validation {
    25	  ok: boolean;
    26	  errors: string[];
    27	}
    28	
    29	const PRED_KEYS = ["version", "caseId", "envelopeHash", "task", "probs", "abstain", "reason"];
    30	const FORBIDDEN = ["__proto__", "constructor", "prototype"];
    31	const isObj = (v: unknown): v is Record<string, unknown> => typeof v === "object" && v !== null && !Array.isArray(v);
    32	
    33	/** The scheduled operations a task asks for: null for the sham, else the list `validateInterventions` takes. Errors name the task, never repair it. */
    34	export function taskSchedule(env: PredictionEnvelope): { schedule: ScheduledIntervention[] | null; errors: string[] } {
    35	  const t = env.task, op = t.op, cfg = env.config, errors: string[] = [];
    36	  const W = worldW(cfg), H = cellCount(cfg) / W;
    37	  const entry = env.menu.ops.find((m) => m.kind === op.kind);
    38	  if (!entry) return { schedule: null, errors: [`operation ${JSON.stringify(op.kind)} is not on the menu`] };
    39	  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(t.delta) || t.delta < env.menu.deltaMin || t.delta > env.menu.deltaMax) errors.push(`delta ${t.delta} is outside the menu's ${env.menu.deltaMin}..${env.menu.deltaMax}`);
    40	  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(t.horizon) || t.horizon < t.delta) errors.push(`horizon ${t.horizon} must be at least delta ${t.delta}`);
    41	  if (op.kind === "none") {
    42	    for (const k of Object.keys(op)) if (k !== "kind") errors.push(`a sham has no key ${JSON.stringify(k)}`);
    43	    return { schedule: null, errors };
    44	  }
    45	  const want = op.kind === "lesion" ? ["kind", "x", "y", "r"] : ["kind", "x", "y", "r", "amount"];
    46	  for (const k of Object.keys(op)) if (!want.includes(k)) errors.push(`a ${op.kind} has no key ${JSON.stringify(k)}`);
    47	  for (const k of want) if (!Object.hasOwn(op, k)) errors.push(`a ${op.kind} needs the key ${JSON.stringify(k)}`);
    48	  if (errors.length) return { schedule: null, errors };
    49	  const { x, y, r } = op as { x: number; y: number; r: number };
    50	  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(r) || r < (entry.rMin ?? 1) || r > (entry.rMax ?? 0)) errors.push(`radius ${r} is outside the menu's ${entry.rMin}..${entry.rMax}`);
    51	  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(x) || x < 0 || x >= W) errors.push(`x ${x} is outside the world`);
    52	  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(y) || y < 0 || y >= H) errors.push(`y ${y} is outside the world`);
    53	  let entryOut: ScheduledIntervention;
    54	  const step = t.forkStep + t.delta;
    55	  if (op.kind === "lesion") entryOut = { step, kind: "lesion", x, y, r };
    56	  else {
    57	    const a = op.amount as number;
    58	    if (!Number.isSafeInteger(a) || a < (entry.amountMin ?? 1) || a > (entry.amountMax ?? 0)) errors.push(`amount ${a} is outside the menu's ${entry.amountMin}..${entry.amountMax}`);
    59	    entryOut = { step, kind: "feed", x, y, r, amount: op.kind === "drain" ? -a : a };
    60	  }
    61	  if (errors.length) return { schedule: null, errors };
    62	  const schedule = [entryOut];
    63	  const bad = validateInterventions(schedule);
    64	  if (bad.length) return { schedule: null, errors: bad };
    65	  const world = interventionsError({ interventions: schedule }, cfg, t.forkStep);
    66	  if (world) return { schedule: null, errors: [world] };
    67	  return { schedule, errors: [] };
    68	}
    69	
    70	/** An envelope must itself be sound: right version, intact hash, a task its own menu allows. Builders assert this; the validator trusts only a sound one. */
    71	export function validateEnvelope(env: PredictionEnvelope): Validation {
    72	  const errors: string[] = [];
    73	  if (env.version !== ENVELOPE_VERSION) errors.push(`envelope version ${env.version}`);
    74	  if (!envelopeIntact(env)) errors.push("envelope hash does not match its content");
    75	  errors.push(...taskSchedule(env).errors);
    76	  return { ok: errors.length === 0, errors };
    77	}
    78	
    79	export function validatePrediction(p: unknown, env: PredictionEnvelope): Validation {
    80	  const errors: string[] = [];
    81	  if (!isObj(p)) return { ok: false, errors: ["a prediction must be a JSON object"] };
    82	  for (const k of Object.keys(p)) {
    83	    if (FORBIDDEN.includes(k)) errors.push(`forbidden key ${JSON.stringify(k)}`);
    84	    else if (!PRED_KEYS.includes(k)) errors.push(`unknown key ${JSON.stringify(k)}`);
    85	  }
    86	  if (errors.length) return { ok: false, errors };
    87	  if (p.version !== PREDICTION_VERSION) errors.push(`version must be ${PREDICTION_VERSION}`);
    88	  if (p.caseId !== env.caseId) errors.push(`caseId ${JSON.stringify(p.caseId)} is not ${JSON.stringify(env.caseId)}`);
    89	  if (p.envelopeHash !== env.envelopeHash) errors.push("envelopeHash does not match the envelope");
    90	  if (!isObj(p.task)) errors.push("task is missing");
    91	  else {
    92	    let same = false;
    93	    try {
    94	      same = canonical(p.task) === canonical(env.task);
    95	    } catch {
    96	      same = false;
    97	    }
    98	    if (!same) errors.push("task does not restate the envelope's task verbatim (the question is fixed by the host)");
    99	  }
   100	  if (typeof p.abstain !== "boolean") errors.push("abstain must be a boolean");
   101	  if (p.reason !== undefined && typeof p.reason !== "string") errors.push("reason must be a string");
   102	  if (p.abstain === true) {
   103	    if (Object.hasOwn(p, "probs")) errors.push("an abstention carries no probs");
   104	  } else if (p.abstain === false) {
   105	    if (!isObj(p.probs)) errors.push("probs are required unless abstaining");
   106	    else {
   107	      const pr = p.probs;
   108	      for (const k of Object.keys(pr)) if (!(CLASSES as readonly string[]).includes(k)) errors.push(`probs has unknown key ${JSON.stringify(k)}`);
   109	      let sum = 0;
   110	      for (const c of CLASSES) {
   111	        const v = pr[c];
   112	        if (!Number.isInteger(v) || (v as number) < 0 || (v as number) > 1000) errors.push(`probs.${c} must be an integer in 0..1000 (per-mille)`);
   113	        else sum += v as number;
   114	      }
   115	      if (errors.length === 0 && sum !== 1000) errors.push(`probs must sum to exactly 1000, got ${sum}`);
   116	    }
   117	  }
   118	  return { ok: errors.length === 0, errors };
   119	}
   120	
   121	/** argmax of the stated probs, tie order none > down > up (derived, never submitted). */
   122	export function argmax(p: Probs): Direction3 {
   123	  let best: Direction3 = "none";
   124	  for (const c of ["none", "down", "up"] as const) if (p[c] > p[best]) best = c;
   125	  return best;
   126	}
   127	
   128	export interface FileCheck {
   129	  /** caseId -> prediction-or-null (null: invalid, or no valid line). */
   130	  byCase: Map<string, { prediction: Prediction | null; errors: string[] }>;
   131	  invalid: number;
   132	  missing: string[];
   133	  extra: string[];
   134	  duplicates: string[];
   135	  notObject: number;
   136	}
   137	
   138	/** File-level validation: exactly one line per case. Extras, duplicates and missing cases are counted; a duplicated case scores as invalid. */
   139	export function validatePredictionFile(lines: string[], envelopes: readonly PredictionEnvelope[]): FileCheck {
   140	  const byEnv = new Map(envelopes.map((e) => [e.caseId, e]));
   141	  const out: FileCheck = { byCase: new Map(), invalid: 0, missing: [], extra: [], duplicates: [], notObject: 0 };
   142	  const seen = new Set<string>();
   143	  for (const line of lines) {
   144	    if (line.trim() === "") continue;
   145	    let raw: unknown;
   146	    try {
   147	      raw = JSON.parse(line);
   148	    } catch {
   149	      out.notObject++;
   150	      out.invalid++;
   151	      continue;
   152	    }
   153	    if (!isObj(raw)) {
   154	      out.notObject++;
   155	      out.invalid++;
   156	      continue;
   157	    }
   158	    const id = typeof raw.caseId === "string" ? raw.caseId : "";
   159	    const env = byEnv.get(id);
   160	    if (!env) {
   161	      out.extra.push(id);
   162	      out.invalid++;
   163	      continue;
   164	    }
   165	    if (seen.has(id)) {
   166	      out.duplicates.push(id);
   167	      out.byCase.set(id, { prediction: null, errors: ["duplicate line for this case"] });
   168	      out.invalid++;
   169	      continue;
   170	    }
   171	    seen.add(id);
   172	    const v = validatePrediction(raw, env);
   173	    if (!v.ok) out.invalid++;
   174	    out.byCase.set(id, { prediction: v.ok ? (raw as unknown as Prediction) : null, errors: v.errors });
   175	  }
   176	  for (const e of envelopes) if (!seen.has(e.caseId)) out.missing.push(e.caseId);
   177	  return out;
   178	}
     1	// The model seam (PLAN.md, section 4): a typed adapter interface for a future model proposer, with no implementation, no network
     2	// import and nothing registered. Raw model output is routed through the host validator and is never repaired. Unrun in this slice.
     3	import type { PredictionEnvelope } from "./envelope.ts";
     4	import { validatePrediction, type Prediction, type Validation } from "./validate.ts";
     5	
     6	export interface ModelContext {
     7	  /** A stable label for the run (model name, prompt version); recorded in meta, never scored. */
     8	  runLabel: string;
     9	}
    10	export interface ModelProposerAdapter {
    11	  id: string;
    12	  /** Returns the raw output: a JSON string or an object. It must be derived from the envelope only. */
    13	  propose(env: PredictionEnvelope, ctx: ModelContext): Promise<unknown>;
    14	}
    15	
    16	/** Adapters registered for a run. Empty by design: this slice runs no model. */
    17	export const REGISTERED_ADAPTERS: readonly ModelProposerAdapter[] = Object.freeze([]);
    18	
    19	export interface Adapted {
    20	  prediction: Prediction | null;
    21	  validation: Validation;
    22	}
    23	
    24	/** Raw output (a JSON string or an object) through the validator. An invalid output yields no prediction and its errors. */
    25	export function adaptModelOutput(raw: unknown, env: PredictionEnvelope): Adapted {
    26	  let value = raw;
    27	  if (typeof raw === "string") {
    28	    try {
    29	      value = JSON.parse(raw);
    30	    } catch {
    31	      return { prediction: null, validation: { ok: false, errors: ["output is not JSON"] } };
    32	    }
    33	  }
    34	  const validation = validatePrediction(value, env);
    35	  return { prediction: validation.ok ? (value as Prediction) : null, validation };
    36	}

     1	// Scoring (PLAN.md, section 8). Multiclass Brier over {up, down, none}: BS = (1/N) sum_i sum_c (p_ic/1000 - o_ic)^2, range 0..2.
     2	// The headline imputes abstentions and invalid/missing predictions with the frozen dev-split prior, so abstaining cannot beat the
     3	// prior; answered-only numbers are reported beside it. A calibrated majority baseline is legitimately strong and is not claimed
     4	// to be punished. The family is the unit of uncertainty.
     5	import { mulberry32 } from "@bl/metrics";
     6	import { CLASSES, type Direction3 } from "./envelope.ts";
     7	import { argmax, type Prediction, type Probs } from "./validate.ts";
     8	
     9	export interface ScoredCase {
    10	  caseId: string;
    11	  familyId: string;
    12	  outcome: Direction3;
    13	  /** null: abstained, invalid or missing. */
    14	  prediction: Prediction | null;
    15	  status: "answered" | "abstained" | "invalid" | "missing";
    16	}
    17	
    18	export const brierOf = (p: Probs, o: Direction3): number => {
    19	  let s = 0;
    20	  for (const c of CLASSES) {
    21	    const d = p[c] / 1000 - (c === o ? 1 : 0);
    22	    s += d * d;
    23	  }
    24	  return s;
    25	};
    26	
    27	export interface Reliability {
    28	  bin: string;
    29	  n: number;
    30	  meanStated: number | null;
    31	  hitRate: number | null;
    32	}
    33	export interface ProposerScore {
    34	  id: string;
    35	  n: number;
    36	  answered: number;
    37	  abstained: number;
    38	  invalid: number;
    39	  missing: number;
    40	  coverage: number;
    41	  abstentionRate: number;
    42	  /** Headline: abstained/invalid/missing imputed with the dev prior. */
    43	  brierImputed: number | null;
    44	  brierAnswered: number | null;
    45	  directionalAccuracy: number | null;
    46	  balancedAccuracy: number | null;
    47	  /** Classes with at least one answered case (the balanced accuracy's denominator). */
    48	  balancedClasses: number;
    49	  reliability: Reliability[];
    50	  validNonNullYield: number | null;
    51	}
    52	
    53	export function reliabilityTable(cases: readonly ScoredCase[]): Reliability[] {
    54	  const bins = [0, 0, 0, 0, 0].map(() => ({ n: 0, stated: 0, hit: 0 }));
    55	  for (const c of cases) {
    56	    if (c.status !== "answered" || !c.prediction?.probs) continue;
    57	    const a = argmax(c.prediction.probs);
    58	    const p = c.prediction.probs[a] / 1000;
    59	    const b = Math.min(4, Math.floor(p * 5));
    60	    bins[b].n++;
    61	    bins[b].stated += p;
    62	    if (a === c.outcome) bins[b].hit++;
    63	  }
    64	  return bins.map((b, i) => ({ bin: `${(i * 0.2).toFixed(1)}-${i === 4 ? "1.0" : ((i + 1) * 0.2).toFixed(1)}`, n: b.n, meanStated: b.n ? b.stated / b.n : null, hitRate: b.n ? b.hit / b.n : null }));
    65	}
    66	
    67	export function scoreProposer(id: string, cases: readonly ScoredCase[], devPrior: Probs, restrictTo?: ReadonlySet<string>): ProposerScore {
    68	  const cs = restrictTo ? cases.filter((c) => restrictTo.has(c.caseId)) : cases;
    69	  const n = cs.length;
    70	  const answered = cs.filter((c) => c.status === "answered");
    71	  let impSum = 0, ansSum = 0, hit = 0;
    72	  for (const c of cs) impSum += c.status === "answered" ? brierOf(c.prediction!.probs!, c.outcome) : brierOf(devPrior, c.outcome);
    73	  for (const c of answered) {
    74	    ansSum += brierOf(c.prediction!.probs!, c.outcome);
    75	    if (argmax(c.prediction!.probs!) === c.outcome) hit++;
    76	  }
    77	  let recSum = 0, recK = 0;
    78	  for (const k of CLASSES) {
    79	    const rows = answered.filter((c) => c.outcome === k);
    80	    if (!rows.length) continue;
    81	    recK++;
    82	    recSum += rows.filter((c) => argmax(c.prediction!.probs!) === k).length / rows.length;
    83	  }
    84	  return {
    85	    id,
    86	    n,
    87	    answered: answered.length,
    88	    abstained: cs.filter((c) => c.status === "abstained").length,
    89	    invalid: cs.filter((c) => c.status === "invalid").length,
    90	    missing: cs.filter((c) => c.status === "missing").length,
    91	    coverage: n ? answered.length / n : 0,
    92	    abstentionRate: n ? cs.filter((c) => c.status === "abstained").length / n : 0,
    93	    brierImputed: n ? impSum / n : null,
    94	    brierAnswered: answered.length ? ansSum / answered.length : null,
    95	    directionalAccuracy: answered.length ? hit / answered.length : null,
    96	    balancedAccuracy: recK ? recSum / recK : null,
    97	    balancedClasses: recK,
    98	    reliability: reliabilityTable(cs),
    99	    validNonNullYield: n ? answered.filter((c) => c.outcome !== "none").length / n : null,
   100	  };
   101	}
   102	
   103	/** Cases that every named comparator answered (the least-covering one's set when nested; else the intersection). */
   104	export function matchedCoverageSet(byProposer: Readonly<Record<string, readonly ScoredCase[]>>): Set<string> {
   105	  let acc: Set<string> | undefined;
   106	  for (const cs of Object.values(byProposer)) {
   107	    const a = new Set<string>(cs.filter((c) => c.status === "answered").map((c) => c.caseId));
   108	    const prev: Set<string> | undefined = acc;
   109	    acc = prev === undefined ? a : new Set<string>([...prev].filter((x) => a.has(x)));
   110	  }
   111	  return acc ?? new Set<string>();
   112	}
   113	
   114	/** Total evaluation cost (simulated cell-steps, every arm, null and failed attempts included) over non-null cases; null when there are none. */
   115	export function costPerUseful(totalCellSteps: number, outcomes: readonly Direction3[]): number | null {
   116	  const k = outcomes.filter((o) => o !== "none").length;
   117	  return k === 0 ? null : totalCellSteps / k;
   118	}
   119	
   120	/** Sign agreement of two effect vectors' labels (cross-seed effect stability); cases are paired by index. */
   121	export function signAgreement(a: readonly Direction3[], b: readonly Direction3[]): { n: number; agree: number; rate: number | null } {
   122	  if (a.length !== b.length) throw new Error("label vectors differ in length");
   123	  let agree = 0;
   124	  for (let i = 0; i < a.length; i++) if (a[i] === b[i]) agree++;
   125	  return { n: a.length, agree, rate: a.length ? agree / a.length : null };
   126	}
   127	
   128	export interface Interval {
   129	  mean: number;
   130	  lo: number;
   131	  hi: number;
   132	}
   133	export const MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS = 10;
   134	
   135	/** Per-family mean of a per-case value. */
   136	export function familyMeans(rows: readonly { familyId: string; v: number }[]): Map<string, number> {
   137	  const acc = new Map<string, { s: number; n: number }>();
   138	  for (const r of rows) {
   139	    const a = acc.get(r.familyId) ?? { s: 0, n: 0 };
   140	    a.s += r.v;
   141	    a.n++;
   142	    acc.set(r.familyId, a);
   143	  }
   144	  return new Map([...acc].map(([k, a]) => [k, a.s / a.n]));
   145	}
   146	
   147	/** Whole-family bootstrap of the mean of per-family values; null below MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS families (descriptive only). */
   148	export function familyBootstrap(perFamily: ReadonlyMap<string, number>, resamples: number, seed: number): Interval | null {
   149	  const v = [...perFamily.keys()].sort().map((k) => perFamily.get(k)!);
   150	  if (v.length < MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS) return null;
   151	  const rng = mulberry32(seed);
   152	  const means: number[] = [];
   153	  for (let r = 0; r < resamples; r++) {
   154	    let s = 0;
   155	    for (let i = 0; i < v.length; i++) s += v[Math.floor(rng() * v.length)];
   156	    means.push(s / v.length);
   157	  }
   158	  means.sort((a, b) => a - b);
   159	  return { mean: v.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / v.length, lo: means[Math.floor(0.025 * resamples)], hi: means[Math.min(resamples - 1, Math.ceil(0.975 * resamples) - 1)] };
   160	}
   161	
   162	/** Paired per-family difference (a - b) of two per-family maps over the families both have. */
   163	export function pairedFamilyDiff(a: ReadonlyMap<string, number>, b: ReadonlyMap<string, number>): Map<string, number> {
   164	  const out = new Map<string, number>();
   165	  for (const [k, x] of a) if (b.has(k)) out.set(k, x - b.get(k)!);
   166	  return out;
   167	}
     1	// Whole-family splits and the leak checks (PLAN.md, section 7). A family is one source history with all its windows and seed
     2	// variants; a split never cuts through one. Ranking is by SHA-256 of salt + family id, so the assignment is deterministic and
     3	// independent of input order. A reserved `qualify` subset of dev is used for all qualification and tuning.
     4	import { sha256Text } from "../propose.ts";
     5	import type { PredictionEnvelope } from "./envelope.ts";
     6	
     7	export type Split = "dev" | "test";
     8	
     9	const rank = (salt: string, id: string): string => sha256Text(`${salt}|${id}`);
    10	
    11	export interface SplitPlan {
    12	  dev: string[];
    13	  test: string[];
    14	  qualify: string[];
    15	}
    16	
    17	/** First `nDev` families by hash rank are dev (the first `nQualify` of those are qualify), the rest test. Lists are in rank order. */
    18	export function assignSplits(familyIds: readonly string[], salt: string, nDev: number, nQualify: number): SplitPlan {
    19	  const uniq = [...new Set(familyIds)];
    20	  if (uniq.length !== familyIds.length) throw new Error("duplicate family ids");
    21	  if (nQualify > nDev || nDev >= uniq.length) throw new Error("need 0 <= nQualify <= nDev < number of families");
    22	  const ranked = uniq.map((id) => ({ id, h: rank(salt, id) })).sort((a, b) => (a.h < b.h ? -1 : a.h > b.h ? 1 : 0)).map((x) => x.id);
    23	  return { dev: ranked.slice(0, nDev), test: ranked.slice(nDev), qualify: ranked.slice(0, nQualify) };
    24	}
    25	
    26	/**
    27	 * Donor assignment for the shuffled-evidence control: a derangement of the families within one split (a cyclic shift of the hash
    28	 * ranking, so no family is its own donor and none crosses a split), frozen into the protocol before any scoring.
    29	 */
    30	export function derangement(familyIds: readonly string[], salt: string): Record<string, string> {
    31	  if (familyIds.length < 2) throw new Error("a derangement needs at least two families");
    32	  const ranked = familyIds.map((id) => ({ id, h: rank(`${salt}|donor`, id) })).sort((a, b) => (a.h < b.h ? -1 : a.h > b.h ? 1 : 0)).map((x) => x.id);
    33	  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
    34	  ranked.forEach((id, i) => (out[id] = ranked[(i + 1) % ranked.length]));
    35	  return out;
    36	}
    37	
    38	export function donorsFor(plan: SplitPlan, salt: string): Record<string, string> {
    39	  return { ...derangement(plan.dev, salt), ...derangement(plan.test, salt) };
    40	}
    41	
    42	export interface DonorCheck {
    43	  ok: boolean;
    44	  reason?: string;
    45	}
    46	/** A donor envelope is acceptable only from a different family and history, in the same split. */
    47	export function donorAllowed(env: Pick<PredictionEnvelope, "familyId" | "historyId">, donor: Pick<PredictionEnvelope, "familyId" | "historyId">, splitOf: (familyId: string) => Split | undefined): DonorCheck {
    48	  if (donor.familyId === env.familyId) return { ok: false, reason: "donor is from the same family" };
    49	  if (donor.historyId === env.historyId) return { ok: false, reason: "donor is another window of the same history" };
    50	  const a = splitOf(env.familyId), b = splitOf(donor.familyId);
    51	  if (a === undefined || b === undefined || a !== b) return { ok: false, reason: "donor is from another split" };
    52	  return { ok: true };
    53	}
    54	
    55	/** Returns the problems found (empty when clean): overlapping families, a family in two splits, bad donors, test envelopes sharing a census with a dev one. */
    56	export function assertNoLeak(plan: SplitPlan, donors: Record<string, string>, envelopes: readonly PredictionEnvelope[]): string[] {
    57	  const errs: string[] = [];
    58	  const dev = new Set(plan.dev), test = new Set(plan.test);
    59	  for (const f of dev) if (test.has(f)) errs.push(`family ${f} is in both dev and test`);
    60	  for (const f of plan.qualify) if (!dev.has(f)) errs.push(`qualify family ${f} is not in dev`);
    61	  const splitOf = (f: string): Split | undefined => (dev.has(f) ? "dev" : test.has(f) ? "test" : undefined);
    62	  for (const [f, d] of Object.entries(donors)) {
    63	    if (f === d) errs.push(`family ${f} is its own donor`);
    64	    else if (splitOf(f) !== splitOf(d)) errs.push(`donor ${d} of ${f} is in another split`);
    65	  }
    66	  for (const f of [...dev, ...test]) if (donors[f] === undefined) errs.push(`family ${f} has no donor`);
    67	  const hist = new Map<string, string>();
    68	  for (const e of envelopes) {
    69	    if (splitOf(e.familyId) === undefined) errs.push(`envelope ${e.caseId} belongs to no split`);
    70	    const prev = hist.get(e.historyId);
    71	    if (prev !== undefined && prev !== e.familyId) errs.push(`history ${e.historyId} appears under two families`);
    72	    hist.set(e.historyId, e.familyId);
    73	  }
    74	  const devCensus = new Set<string>();
    75	  for (const e of envelopes) if (dev.has(e.familyId)) devCensus.add(JSON.stringify(e.census));
    76	  for (const e of envelopes) if (test.has(e.familyId) && devCensus.has(JSON.stringify(e.census))) errs.push(`test envelope ${e.caseId} carries a dev census`);
    77	  return errs;
    78	}
     1	// Deterministic baseline proposers (PLAN.md, section 4). A proposer takes only an envelope and returns a prediction (never
     2	// outcomes, never another case's task). Constants are frozen in protocol.json and passed in; nothing here reads a file or a clock.
     3	import { RULES, type Prediction as RulesPrediction } from "../propose.ts";
     4	import { PREDICTION_VERSION, taskKey, type CensusPoint, type Direction3, type PredictionEnvelope } from "./envelope.ts";
     5	import { donorAllowed, type Split } from "./splits.ts";
     6	import type { Prediction, Probs } from "./validate.ts";
     7	
     8	export interface Proposer {
     9	  id: string;
    10	  /** baseline: a rule a model has to beat; control: a deliberately uninformed run; oracle: knows the mechanism, a dev diagnostic only. */
    11	  role: "baseline" | "control" | "oracle";
    12	  propose(env: PredictionEnvelope): Prediction;
    13	}
    14	
    15	export interface ProposerParams {
    16	  fixedRule: { lead: number };
    17	  numericTrend: { window: number; minRelPerMille: number; lead: number; mid: number; low: number };
    18	  v0Rules: { lead: number };
    19	  mechanismRule: { lead: number; lowDoseQuanta: number; scarceNutrient: number };
    20	}
    21	
    22	export function predictionOf(env: PredictionEnvelope, probs: Probs | null, reason: string): Prediction {
    23	  const base = { version: PREDICTION_VERSION, caseId: env.caseId, envelopeHash: env.envelopeHash, task: env.task, reason };
    24	  return probs ? { ...base, probs, abstain: false } : { ...base, abstain: true };
    25	}
    26	
    27	/** `lead` per-mille on `dir`, the rest split evenly between the other two (the first, in order up/down/none, takes any odd remainder). */
    28	export function leadProbs(dir: Direction3, lead: number): Probs {
    29	  const rest = 1000 - lead;
    30	  const half = Math.floor(rest / 2);
    31	  const out: Probs = { up: 0, down: 0, none: 0 };
    32	  const others = (["up", "down", "none"] as const).filter((c) => c !== dir);
    33	  out[dir] = lead;
    34	  out[others[0]] = rest - half;
    35	  out[others[1]] = half;
    36	  return out;
    37	}
    38	
    39	/** Largest-remainder rounding of non-negative weights to per-mille summing to exactly 1000. */
    40	export function toPerMille(w: Record<Direction3, number>): Probs {
    41	  const tot = w.up + w.down + w.none;
    42	  if (!(tot > 0)) throw new Error("weights must have a positive sum");
    43	  const cls = ["up", "down", "none"] as const;
    44	  const raw = cls.map((c) => (w[c] * 1000) / tot);
    45	  const fl = raw.map(Math.floor);
    46	  let left = 1000 - fl.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
    47	  const order = raw.map((r, i) => ({ i, f: r - fl[i] })).sort((a, b) => b.f - a.f || a.i - b.i);
    48	  for (const o of order) {
    49	    if (left <= 0) break;
    50	    fl[o.i]++;
    51	    left--;
    52	  }
    53	  return { up: fl[0], down: fl[1], none: fl[2] };
    54	}
    55	
    56	export const fixedRule = (k: ProposerParams["fixedRule"]): Proposer => ({
    57	  id: "fixedRule",
    58	  role: "baseline",
    59	  propose(env) {
    60	    const kind = env.task.op.kind;
    61	    const dir: Direction3 = kind === "feed" ? "up" : kind === "lesion" || kind === "drain" ? "down" : "none";
    62	    return predictionOf(env, leadProbs(dir, k.lead), `fixed rule: ${kind} -> ${dir}`);
    63	  },
    64	});
    65	
    66	/** The census-only trend: slope of the primary readout over the last `window` samples, extrapolated over the horizon, as a share of the current level. Ignores frames and the task. */
    67	export function trendDirection(census: readonly CensusPoint[], horizon: number, k: ProposerParams["numericTrend"]): Direction3 {
    68	  if (census.length < k.window + 1) return "none";
    69	  const last = census[census.length - 1], first = census[census.length - 1 - k.window];
    70	  const span = last.step - first.step;
    71	  if (span <= 0) return "none";
    72	  // change over the horizon, per-mille of the current level, in integer arithmetic.
    73	  const delta = (last.mass - first.mass) * horizon * 1000;
    74	  const bar = k.minRelPerMille * Math.max(last.mass, 1) * span;
    75	  return delta >= bar ? "up" : delta <= -bar ? "down" : "none";
    76	}
    77	function trendProbs(dir: Direction3, k: ProposerParams["numericTrend"]): Probs {
    78	  if (dir === "none") return { up: k.low, down: k.low, none: 1000 - 2 * k.low };
    79	  const other: Direction3 = dir === "up" ? "down" : "up";
    80	  const out: Probs = { up: 0, down: 0, none: k.mid };
    81	  out[dir] = k.lead;
    82	  out[other] = 1000 - k.lead - k.mid;
    83	  return out;
    84	}
    85	export const numericTrend = (k: ProposerParams["numericTrend"]): Proposer => ({
    86	  id: "numericTrend",
    87	  role: "baseline",
    88	  propose(env) {
    89	    const d = trendDirection(env.census, env.task.horizon, k);
    90	    return predictionOf(env, trendProbs(d, k), `census trend -> ${d}`);
    91	  },
    92	});
    93	
    94	/**
    95	 * The shuffled-evidence control: the numeric-trend rule run on a donor family's census. The donor is assigned by the frozen
    96	 * derangement; a donor from the same family, the same history (a later window of it) or another split is refused (throws), so a
    97	 * control can never be handed the case's own future.
    98	 */
    99	export function shuffledEvidence(
   100	  k: ProposerParams["numericTrend"],
   101	  donors: Readonly<Record<string, string>>,
   102	  envelopeOf: (familyId: string) => PredictionEnvelope | undefined,
   103	  splitOf: (familyId: string) => Split | undefined,
   104	): Proposer {
   105	  return {
   106	    id: "shuffledEvidence",
   107	    role: "control",
   108	    propose(env) {
   109	      const donorFamily = donors[env.familyId];
   110	      const donor = donorFamily === undefined ? undefined : envelopeOf(donorFamily);
   111	      if (!donor) throw new Error(`no donor envelope for family ${env.familyId}`);
   112	      const ok = donorAllowed(env, donor, splitOf);
   113	      if (!ok.ok) throw new Error(`donor refused for ${env.caseId}: ${ok.reason}`);
   114	      const d = trendDirection(donor.census, env.task.horizon, k);
   115	      return predictionOf(env, trendProbs(d, k), `donor census trend -> ${d}`);
   116	    },
   117	  };
   118	}
   119	
   120	export const priorMajority = (devPrior: Probs): Proposer => ({
   121	  id: "priorMajority",
   122	  role: "baseline",
   123	  propose: (env) => predictionOf(env, { ...devPrior }, "dev-split class frequencies"),
   124	});
   125	export const alwaysNone = (): Proposer => ({ id: "alwaysNone", role: "baseline", propose: (env) => predictionOf(env, { up: 0, down: 0, none: 1000 }, "always none") });
   126	export const abstainAll = (): Proposer => ({ id: "abstainAll", role: "baseline", propose: (env) => predictionOf(env, null, "abstains on every case") });
   127	
   128	/** The v0 rule proposer's directional/replay prediction mapped into the schema: 600 on the stated direction, the remainder on none (explicit, versioned). */
   129	export const RULES_MAP_VERSION = 1;
   130	export function fromRulesProposal(env: PredictionEnvelope, pred: RulesPrediction, lead: number): Prediction {
   131	  const probs: Probs = pred.kind === "directional" && pred.direction ? { up: 0, down: 0, none: 1000 - lead, [pred.direction]: lead } as Probs : { up: 0, down: 0, none: 1000 };
   132	  return predictionOf(env, probs, `rules-v0 (map v${RULES_MAP_VERSION}): ${pred.kind}${pred.direction ? " " + pred.direction : ""}`);
   133	}
   134	export const v0Rules = (k: ProposerParams["v0Rules"]): Proposer => ({
   135	  id: "v0Rules",
   136	  role: "baseline",
   137	  propose(env) {
   138	    // The v0 rule table (tools/lib/propose.ts RULES): feed and drain rows from the nutrient-held signals, lesion from the standing-body signals, control otherwise.
   139	    const kind = env.task.op.kind;
   140	    const row = kind === "feed" ? RULES.livingCells.low : kind === "drain" ? RULES.livingCells.high : kind === "lesion" ? RULES.lineages.high : RULES.lineages.low;
   141	    const pred: RulesPrediction = row.action === "control" ? { kind: "replay", statement: "replays" } : { kind: "directional", statement: `${row.action}`, direction: row.direction };
   142	    return fromRulesProposal(env, pred, k.lead);
   143	  },
   144	});
   145	
   146	/** Positive control, a dev diagnostic only: knows the fixture mechanisms (lesion centre occupancy from the frames, dose against the nutrient in the field). Never a baseline to beat. */
   147	export const mechanismRule = (k: ProposerParams["mechanismRule"]): Proposer => ({
   148	  id: "mechanismRule",
   149	  role: "oracle",
   150	  propose(env) {
   151	    const op = env.task.op;
   152	    const last = env.frames[env.frames.length - 1];
   153	    const cen = env.census[env.census.length - 1];
   154	    const block = (x: number, y: number): number => {
   155	      const w = env.config.tileW * env.config.tilesX;
   156	      const bs = Math.floor(w / last.side);
   157	      return last.mass[Math.floor(y / bs) * last.side + Math.floor(x / bs)];
   158	    };
   159	    let dir: Direction3 = "none";
   160	    if (op.kind === "lesion") dir = block(op.x!, op.y!) > 0 ? "down" : "none";
   161	    else if (op.kind === "feed") dir = (op.r! * op.r! * 3 * op.amount!) >= k.lowDoseQuanta ? "up" : "none";
   162	    else if (op.kind === "drain") dir = cen.freeNutrient >= k.scarceNutrient ? "down" : "none";
   163	    return predictionOf(env, leadProbs(dir, k.lead), `mechanism: ${op.kind} -> ${dir}`);
   164	  },
   165	});
   166	
   167	export const caseKey = (env: PredictionEnvelope): string => taskKey(env.task);
